4. COMP 23-001 Update to Navajo County Comprehensive Plan: Consideration of an update to the
1 of 5 
DRAFT

<!-- page 381 -->
4. COMP 23-001 Update to Navajo County Comprehensive Plan: Consideration of an update to the
Comprehensive Plan of Navajo County:
Mr. Cody Cooper, Planning Manager, presented the item to the Commission.
The update would include:
  
A hearing process for proposing major amendments to the Navajo County Comprehensive Plan. Mr.
Cooper then gave a brief overview of how that process would function.
Updated population numbers and estimates to reflect the 2020 Census results, as well as more current
information and guidance provided from the Arizona Office of Economic Opportunity.
Formatting to make the Comprehensive Plan less complex and improve readability, remove redundancy,
and update the goals that have been met or need to be adjusted for clarity.
Mr. Cooper moved on to describing the public outreach Staff had done, a 60-day commitment period that
began in January. In total, 15 adjacent jurisdictions had been contacted, and three responded with questions,
but had no comments or concerns about the update. Two public meetings had been hosted, one in Holbrook
and the other in Show Low. No members of the public attended or provided comments at either meeting. With
this information, Staff recommends approval of this update.
There were no comments from the attending public about the Comprehensive Plan.
Commissioner Murph asked where the numbers presented were from. Mr. Cooper clarified that the models
were from the Arizona Office of Economic Opportunity, and that they were making projections for growth
based on their updated data, as the last models were flawed. 
Commissioner Murph continued, stating that even knowing that the projections were not set in stone, they
would still influence the County in some way. Mr. Cooper replied that they could, and that while they were
going to plan for the current citizens of the County, they also needed to be flexible to account for any changes
that may happen in the future.
With no further comments from the Commission or the public, Commissioner Murph made a motion to
recommend approval to the Board of Supervisors. Commissioner Rogers seconded the motion. Motion
passed, 8 - 0.
 
  
 
5. SUP 23-001 White Mountain Lake Self-Storage - District III:  Consideration of a request for a Special Use
Permit to develop a self-storage facility with four banks of new storage units on a vacant 1.2-acre parcel
within the White Mountain Lake area of Show Low:
Ms. Sondra Starke, Planner I, presented the item to the Commission. The request was for a self-storage
rental facility on Silver Creek Drive in White Mountain Lakes with four banks of storage. Ms. Starke showed
the Commission a map of the surrounding area and listed the nearby landmarks.  She then talked about the
features of the project that met development standards such as the ingress and egress, engineering, fire
safety requirements, drainage detention, etc.
The zoning for the property is Commercial-Residential (C-R) and does allow storage facilities with a Special
Use Permit. Staff had not received any comments from the public either for or against the project. With these
findings of fact, Staff recommended approval of the Special Use Permit.
The applicant, Mr. Joseph Holland, addressed the Commission. Mr. Holland said that the community was very
much in need of more storage facilities and felt that his project would help meet those demands.
Mr. Ronald Longenbaugh asked the Commission about the growth of the area and if there would be more
commercial uses, as he was under the impression the area was residential. He had no comments either for or
against the project.
Mr. Cody Cooper, Planning Manager, addressed Mr. Longenbaugh's question, showing the Commission and
Mr. Longenbaugh the areas that were zoned C-R and stated that it did have the potential for more commercial
uses.
With there being no further comments from either the public or the Commission, Commissioner Muder made
a motion to recommend approval to the Board. Commissioner Rogers seconded the motion. Motion passed
unanimously, 8 - 0.
  
 
6. SUP 23-002 Kennison Contractor Yard - District IV: Consideration of a request for a Special Use Permit to   
2 of 5 
DRAFT

<!-- page 382 -->
6. SUP 23-002 Kennison Contractor Yard - District IV: Consideration of a request for a Special Use Permit to
expand use of an existing primary dwelling to include indoor storage of contractor’s equipment on a 0.44-acre
parcel located in Overgaard. APN# 206-27-020, T12N, R17E, S33. Owner: Kennison Brian R & Silvia M
Family Revocable Trust. Agent: Brian Kennison:
Ms. Sondra Starke, Planner I, presented the item to the Commission. The property owner, Mr. Brian Kennison
was requesting a Special Use Permit (SUP) to add a use to his permitted single-family dwelling as a storage
garage for his landscaping business’ equipment. 
The property is just to the west of Bison Ranch, and the zoning for the property is R1-10. The zoning does
allow for contractor's yards, with the condition that they obtain an SUP. Ms. Starke further shared with the
Commission a site plan of the project and explained that the permitted structure would have an 800 square
foot living quarters inside of it, and then 1600 square feet for equipment storage. 
The use is compatible with the surrounding area, as the property is adjacent to C-R (Commercial Residential)
zoning along Highway 260. The project has been determined to be compatible with current and future uses
and fits in with the Comprehensive Plan. With these findings of fact, Staff recommended approval.
The applicant, Brian Kennison, addressed the Commission and thanked them for their time. He stated that the
reason they chose this property was because of the Assessor's web description of the list of allowed uses,
one of which was a contractor's yard, with an asterisk attached to it. Mr. Kennison also mentioned that the
location was ideal for his business as it was very close to Bison Ranch, which he had contracts with for 18
years to maintain their grounds and roads.
Mr. Kennison had not inquired further than the list of allowed uses and moved his equipment onto the
property. Following a complaint, he was informed by Code Enforcement that he was required to obtain a
Special Use Permit.
Vice-Chairwoman Smith opened the meeting for public comment.
There were no comments in favor of the project.
Comments against the project:
Sandy Kreston, Jeff Schwoerer, John Schusler, and Kim Johnston all had comments against the project,
including:
  
The yard was currently a mess, with equipment and materials scattered around, and had been that way
for two to three years.
Concern over code violations.
The lot did not have a home on it.
The current screening was not sufficient.
The building proposed would not be big enough to hold the equipment.
The business was bringing down property values.
Noise of employees and machinery.
Hours of Operation.
Invasive lighting.
Concern over who would be living on the property.
Concern over harmful chemicals being stored on the property.
Concern that the equipment would damage the roads and the easement used to access the property.
There would be gravel and dirt on the roads because of the equipment that made walking or biking
difficult.
Vice-Chairwoman Smith asked Mr. Kennison to address some of these comments.
Mr. Kennison responded:
  
The building would be arriving next Monday, and groundbreaking would begin in April, shielding the
closest neighbor's view from his back deck.
The yard would be screened with trees and landscaping, and the current screening was temporary.
His hours of operation for his business would be from 8:00 AM to 4:00 PM. Most work would be done
off-site, with employees only picking up equipment in the morning and returning it by the end of the day.
The easement in question was used by two other commercial businesses.
He would not be storing harmful chemicals or materials.
Mr. Kennison also commented that it was likely he would rent the living quarters to one of his
employees, of which he had two.
Current materials on the property were for the construction of the building and he would no longer be
storing materials on site once construction was complete.
Any damage to the roads because of the equipment would be repaired by his company, as he was
under contract with Bison Ranch to maintain them.
He would not be doing retail business on site.
  
3 of 5 
DRAFT

<!-- page 383 -->
Repairs would be done inside of the building, not outside. Mr. Kennison also had an off-site mechanic to
which would take the equipment.
The structure is permitted by Navajo County.
All the non-compliant equipment will be moved to a different property.
Vice-Chairwoman Smith asked about the easement being blocked. Mr. Kennison replied that the easement
back behind the auto body shop towards the Dollar General had been blocked for roughly ten years.
Vice-Chairwoman Smith also provided comments about the storing of materials and parking of private vehicles
on the easement close to the fire hydrant and requested that they be moved for public safety, to which Mr.
Kennison agreed.
Commissioner Rogers asked about any Code Enforcement violations. Mr. Cody Cooper, Planning Manager,
responded saying that there was an open Code Enforcement violation on the property, which is why Mr.
Kennison was applying for this SUP to resolve the issues. The violation was the outdoor storage of
contractors' materials, as well as the utilization of the contractors' yard.  Code Enforcement stated that the
applicant either needed to acquire an SUP or cease the usage in order to close the open violation. 
Commissioner Murph asked for clarification of the need for the SUP process, what was by-right usage, and
when the line was drawn to have to apply for an SUP, as the building permit had been issued. Mr. Cooper
noted that the hearing process is in place for Staff to be able to ensure that the use fits in with the
Comprehensive Plan, and that public interests and safety be considered in a public forum.
Mr. Mischa Larisch, Public Works Deputy Director of Planning & Development Services, approached the
Commission to state the reason Mr. Kennison’s use was not by-right was because he had outside employees
come to the property. Otherwise, if he had only his family running the business, he would be free to store the
equipment inside of his building without an SUP.
Commissioner Muder asked if storage containers were allowed in the R1-10 zoning. Mr Cooper replied that
they were allowed under certain parameters in the Zoning Ordinance.
There was more discussion regarding fencing, with Commissioner Rogers asking if they could add in
a stipulation that he fence the property. Mr. Cooper responded that the Commission could add in that
requirement but could only go so far as to require a solid fence, not material types.
Vice-Chairwoman Smith asked Staff to clarify the 12-month sunset clause on the SUP. Mr. Cooper
addressed this, stating that the applicant had 12 months to start construction, or the permit would expire.
Commissioner Howe made a motion to recommend approval to the Board, with an addition that a fence be
put in per staff recommendation to shield them from the neighbors on the east. Commissioner Rogers
seconded the motion. Motion passed, 7 - 1 (Commissioner Booher).
 
7. SUP-A 23-001 Evergreen Storage - District IV:  Consideration of a request for a Special Use Permit
Amendment from an RV Park and RV storage facility to a two-phase RV, trailer, and mini-storage facility on a
vacant 4.30-acre parcel in the Overgaard area.  APN# 206-02-012C, T12N, R17E, S30. Owner: Larry and
Dianne Holland. Applicant/Agent: Logan Rogers:
Both Vice-Chairwoman Smith and Commissioner Rogers temporarily recused themselves from the hearing
for this item, as they both had a conflict of interest in the project, with Commissioner Rogers being the
applicant and Vice-Chairwoman Smith being the representative for the real-estate transaction.
Per the bylaws of the Commission, members needed to elect a Chair Pro Tem to proceed with the meeting
and continue.
Commissioner Murph nominated Commissioner Howe as Chair Pro tem; Commissioner Muder seconded
the motion. The vote was unanimous, 6 - 0, for the election of Commissioner Howe as Pro tem.
With this business concluded, Mr. Cody Cooper, Planning Manager, presented the item to the Commission,
giving them a brief history of the project, stating that this was an amendment to an existing Special Use Permit
that had been approved by the Board of Supervisors in October of 2022. 
The approval was given to the current landowner, with a different applicant, with the condition that the project
be started within twelve (12) months or the permit would expire. This meant that the previous approval was
still active. The current applicant was requesting an amendment with their own proposal for the property,
which would have two phases. The original approval was for 20 to 25 RV Park sites and 20 storage units. 
The amended use proposes that Phase One be 200 self-storage units with 44 RV and trailer storage spaces.
Phase two would then remove the RV and trailer storage, add an additional 100 storage units, and add a
commercial center. Mr. Cooper showed the area of notification for this amendment, noting that there was no
public response either for or against the project. Staff recommended approval of the amendment.
Applicant, Logan Rogers, addressed the Commission, stating that the reason he took on this project was to
  
4 of 5 
DRAFT

<!-- page 384 -->
Applicant, Logan Rogers, addressed the Commission, stating that the reason he took on this project was to
serve a need for more storage facilities in the community as the current storage facilities in the area are at full
capacity and have been so for years. His reason for requesting the two-phase project was to minimize the
need to amend another permit, as they were planning for future expansion.
With there being no further comments from the Commission or the public, Commissioner Muder made a
motion to recommend approval to the Board. Commissioner Murph seconded the motion. Motion carried
unanimously, 6 - 0.

 Re:  Narrative of Investment in White Mountain Lake, AZ 
 
Dear Berrin: 
 
I am the qualifying party for a licensed contractor, Mountain Construction Management LLC (ROC 
#333835).  A partner and I acquired parcel numbers 304-19-584 and 304-19-585 (since merged to 304-19-
584A), which are zoned Commercial -Residential.  Recognizing that there are no self -storage facilities in 
White Mountain Lake (only a few miles west on the road coming into White Mountain Lake or some units 
along Bourden Ranch Road) and  recognizing a general sh ortage of self -storage facilities in general, we 
would like to install a self-storage facility on these parcels. 
 
We know that because the project is cumulatively more than an acre, that we will need to address 
drainage and access and have been working with Painted Sky Engineering and Survey to put together a site 
plan.  They have assured us that we have adequate drainage detention and that our project will have ample 
access and setbacks for the area.  
 
As is shown on the  attached site plan, we plan to erect  four banks of new storage facilities. The 
banks may be constructed of stand -alone buildings or storage containers, as is commonly used in the 
industry.  The South, East, and West sides of the property will be fenced with 6’ chain link and the North 
side that abuts the residential ly zoned lots will be a solid fe nce that meets Navajo County standar ds. We 
intend to install entrances on the east and west side along Silver Creek Drive with automatic gates operated 
by keypads at each entrance. The entrances from Silver Creek Drive will be paved to the property line and 
a 50-foot stacking area will be provided inside of the property at each entrance for the patrons of the facility 
to safely pull off the street while entering the site. 
 
We do not envision providing electricity to the individual units, but  solar operated motion-sensor 
lights will be installed on the corners of each building.  No water or sewer facilities will be necessary,  and 
the units will not be insulated.  There is a fire hydrant maintained by Voyager Water on the corner of Silver 
Creek Drive and Bourdon Street, which we be lieve provides adequate water pressure to suppress fires, 
should such become necessary. 
 
If you have any questions or concerns, please feel free to contact me. 
 
Yours very truly,

Page 1 
 
I. INTRODUCTION 
 
The White Mountain Lake Self Storage project is located in Show Low, Arizona at 
8348 Silver Creek Drive. The site is located on parcel number 304-19-584A which 
is 1.2 acres in size. The site is currently vacant. The proposed improvements to 
the site include self-storage units and RV/trailer parking. 
  
The purpose of this report is to provide the results of the preliminary drainage 
analysis that was performed, estimating the anticipated flows for the 2-year, 10-
year, 50-year and 100-year storm events for the proposed conditions and provide 
calculations for handling these flows.  
 
 
 
 
Figure 1. Vicinity Map 
 
 
II. METHODOLOGY 
 
The analysis methodology was based on the rational method, shown in Equation 1 below. 
The rational method was used to estimate peak flow rates for the storm events. 
Runoff coefficients for the site were determined using the ADOT Highway 
Drainage Design Manual-Volume 2. 
 
SITE

<!-- page 405 -->
Page 2 
 
Equation 1. The Rational Method 
 
Q=C*I*A 
 
Q= Peak Flow Rate (cfs) 
C= Runoff Coefficient (C) 
I= Rainfall Intensity (inches per hour) 
A= Area (Acres) 
 
The peak flow rates were estimated using the AutoCAD Civil 3D “Hydraflow 
Hydrographs Extension” to route the flows through the site and to estimate the 
peak flows for the 2-year, 10-year, 50-year and 100-year events at specific 
concentration points. Rainfall data was determined using the NOAA Atlas 14 
website for Atlas 14, Volume 1, Version 5 near the project site.  
 
Autodesk’s Hydraflow Hydrographs Extension was used to create hydrographs. 
Hydrographs were then used to route flows through the site.  
 
The analysis developed flow rates for the pre and post flow rates for the 2, 10, 50 
and 100-year events for the project.  
 
The drainage area was delineated from recent mapping for the site and from field 
observations.  
 
 
III. EXISTING CONDITIONS 
 
The site is located just off of Silver Creek Drive, in White Mountain Lakes Estates. 
The site generally drains in two directions, the North half drains towards the 
Northwest corner, and the South half drains towards the Southwest corner. Figure 
2 shows how the existing drainage flows across the site with flow arrows placed 
perpendicular to the existing contours. 
 
Two drainage areas were determined for the site, one for the area that drains to 
the Northwest corner of the site and the other for the area that drains to the 
Southwest corner of the site. These drainage areas can be seen in Figure 4 in the 
Appendix. 
 
A weighted runoff coefficient “C” was determined for the existing site. To 
determine the weighted runoff coefficient, each area with a different surface 
material was assigned a C-value. It was determined that a C-value of 0.55 would 
be used for the areas with native grasses and juniper trees, 0.78 was used for the 
areas of existing gravel or cinder roads/driveways, and 0.95 was used for any 
impervious surfaces.

<!-- page 406 -->
Page 3 
 
Due to the size of the drainage areas that were analyzed, the minimum time of 
concentration of 10 minutes was used for this analysis. 
 
The existing peak flows leaving the site were determined and are shown below. 
 
• 2- year = 5.5 cfs 
• 10-year = 8.7 cfs 
• 50-year = 12.1 cfs 
• 100-year = 13.7 cfs 
 
Figure 2. Existing Site 
 
 
IV. PROPOSED CONDITIONS 
 
The proposed improvements to the site include 4 rows of self-storage buildings, 
two paved entrances into the site, gravel roads inside the site, and a detention 
Site

<!-- page 407 -->
Page 4 
 
basin. Figure 3 in the Appendix shows the proposed layout for the improvements 
to the site. 
 
New drainage areas were determined for the post-development conditions of the 
site. These drainage areas can be seen in Figure 5 in the Appendix. 
 
A weighted runoff coefficient “C” was determined for the post-development 
drainage from the site. To determine the weighted runoff coefficient, the same C-
values used for the existing drainage were again used and the area for each 
surface were adjusted to account for the additional impervious surface.  
  
For the proposed site analysis, the minimum time of concentration of 10 minutes 
was also used. 
 
• 2- year = 5.7 cfs 
• 10-year = 8.9 cfs 
• 50-year = 12.5 cfs 
• 100-year = 14.1 cfs 
The proposed detention basin will be located on the South side of the site, near 
Silver Creek Drive. The bottom elevation of the detention basin shall be 6032.50 
feet, and the top of the basin shall be 6035.00 feet. The total available storage in 
the detention basin shall be 3,012 cubic feet. The basin shall outlet through two 
12-inch culverts at the West end of the basin. 
 
Utilizing the detention basin, the proposed flows off the site were estimated to be 
as follows:  
 
• 2- year = 5.1 cfs 
• 10- year = 7.6 cfs 
• 50-year = 10.2 cfs 
• 100-year = 11.4 cfs 
 
When the existing flows are compared to the proposed flows, it can be seen that 
all of the proposed flows are less than the existing flows, because of the detention 
effect.  
 
VI. SUMMARY 
 
The construction of the new White Mountain Lake Self Storage project will cause 
an increase in storm water runoff from the site. A detention basin will be

<!-- page 408 -->
Page 5 
 
constructed to mitigate the increase in on-site stormwater runoff flows. As 
demonstrated, the project will result in an overall decreased rate of stormwater 
runoff from the site.

STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: May 23, 2023 
Case No:  SUP 23-001 
Action:   Special Use Permit 
Applicant/Owner: Joseph Holland 
Project Name: White Mountain Lake Self-Storage 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
Recommendation: Staff, as well as the Planning and Zoning Commission, recommend approval of 
the Special Use Permit, subject to the conditions of this Staff Report. 
 
Location: The property is located in the White Mountain Lake area of Show Low on APN #  
304-19-584A. 
 
Size: 1.20 acres 
 
Supervisor District: III 
 
Zoning: C-R (Commercial-Residential Zoning District) 
 
Property Owners: 2 Guy Investments LLC 
 
Applicant: Joseph Holland 
 
Representative: Joseph Holland 
 
REQUEST DESCRIPTION:  
A request for a Special Use Permit to develop a self -storage facility with four banks of new storage 
units on a vacant 1.2-acre parcel within the White Mountain Lake area of Show Low.   
 
GENERAL CHARACTER OF NEIGHBORHOOD:  
 
Land to the west, east, and south of the subject property are also zoned Commercial-Residential (C-R).  
To the north of this property are vacant parcels, which are zoned RU-1. 
 
BACKGROUND/HISTORY:

<!-- page 422 -->
Board of Supervisors              2 | P a g e  
 
 
There is no relevant background or history for this project.  
 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION:  
 
This project is designed to contain the following components: 
 
- Four (4) banks of storage facilities, ranging from twenty feet by one hundred and sixty feet 
(20’x160’) to twenty feet by one hundred and eighty-four feet (20’x184’) 
- Two (2) fifty foot (50’) stacking areas 
- Detention basin on the south side of the property 
- Chain link fence six feet (6’) in height on east, south, and west sides of the development 
- Solid fence six feet (6’) in height on north side of development 
- Gravel driveway with fire truck access through the property with keypad entry and Knox key 
switch 
 
ZONING DISTRICTS: 
ARTICLE 15 – (C-R) COMMERCIAL-RESIDENTIAL ZONING DISTRICT 
 
Section 1501 – Purpose.  
 
The principal purpose of this zoning district is to provide for shops and services in convenient locations 
to meet the daily needs of families in the immediate residential neighborhoods and wholesaling or 
distribution activities in locations where there is adequate access to major streets or highways.  Principal 
uses permitted in this zoning district include retail or wholesale commerce, commercial entertainment, 
and some uses of a light manufacturing nature. 
 
ZONING ORDINANCE: 
Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments 
 
Section 2001 – Special Uses 
  
The Board of Supervisors may permit as a Special Use the following uses in zoning districts 
from which they are otherwise prohibited by this Ordinance, unless any such use is further 
prohibited by this Article. 
 
35. Warehousing and storage, mini-storage, including outdoor storage, i.e., recreational 
and equipment storage, public utilities, but not including the warehousing and storage of 
explosives or junk. 
 
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN/AREA PLAN:  
Comprehensive Plan 
 
Land Use Element

<!-- page 423 -->
Board of Supervisors              3 | P a g e  
 
Goal 6: Maintain compatible land use patterns while encouraging free market development. 
 
Policy 6-B: Consider, if needed, providing for buffers between different land uses including, but 
not limited to, setbacks, walls or fences, and landscaping. 
 
- In order to create a buffer between this property and the RU-1 properties located to the north 
of the project, the applicant has included plans for a solid material fence six feet (6’) in height 
on the northern side of the property. 
 
Policy 6-C: Require paved or other appropriately surfaced access to commercial and industrial 
uses. 
 
- The applicant has  Silver Creek Drive , an asphaltic concrete  paved roadway, indicated as 
their point of access for this project. 
 
Goal 7: Facilitate commercial and industrial development, including business and technology 
uses, where appropriate to increase the sales tax and employment opportunities. 
 
Policy 7 -A: Encourage strategic placement of commercial development  so as to attract and 
locate shopping and employment uses that have paved or appropriately surfaced access and are 
easily accessible, particularly in or near established communities and cities, and in communities 
with air and/or rail access. 
 
- The applicant identified in their application narrative that there is curr ently a general 
shortage of self-storage facilities in White Mountain Lake and seeks to bring that business 
into the community.  
 
Circulation Element 
 
Goal 4: Provide for safe travel and access to property. 
 
Policy 4-B: Continue working with developers and property owners to achieve safe and legal 
access for all properties. 
 
- The applicant has shown their access coming from county-maintained roadway Silver Creek 
Drive.   
 
Analysis: 
 
This project meets all relevant goals and policies of the Navajo County Comprehensive Plan.  The 
majority of goals and policies met are related to access, which is indicated to be from Silver Creek 
Drive.  Navajo County’s Comprehensive Plan encourages commercial developments to be located along 
paved access in/near established communities, such as White Mountain Lake. 
 
The applicant has indicated that they will buffer the northern side of their property with a solid material 
six-foot fence, which keeps them in compliance with both the Navajo County Comprehensive Plan and 
the Navajo County Zoning Ordinance.

<!-- page 424 -->
Board of Supervisors              4 | P a g e  
 
As of May 10th, 2023, Planning and Development Services Staff have not received any comments in 
favor of, nor against, the proposed project. 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes 
and Article 29.  
 
This item was posted in the May 5th, 2023 edition of the White Mountain Independent.  Postcards 
were mailed to neighbors within 300 feet (300’) of the property and the subject property was posted 
with public hearing notices. 
 
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
The proposed project meets a variety of goals within Navajo County’s Comprehensive Plan , as 
noted within this report. 
 
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in which the 
property is located. 
 
The proposed use is not allowed by -right in the zoning district in which the property is located, 
which is why a Special Use Permit is being requested.  However, properties to the east, south, and 
west of this property are also zoned Commercial-Residential, which should be compatible with this 
proposed usage. 
 
4. The proposed use is  compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in the 
vicinity of the proposed use.  
 
The proposed use is compatible with those that currently exist in its vicinity.  The applicant has 
indicated screening via a solid six-foot fence on the north side of their property.  This will provide 
a buffer between this proposal and potential single -family residential uses that could be developed 
to the north in the future. 
 
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
There should  be minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general welfare of 
properties in the vicinity of the proposed use.

PLANNING AND DEVELOPMENT SERVICES RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff recommend approval of the Special Use Permit, 
subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. The applicant shall obtain any required Navajo County permits, including but not limited to 
building, grading, drainage, and sign permits. 
 
2. No building or structure shall be occupied prior to compliance with all appropriate Building 
Division requirements. 
 
3. All lighting on the property shall be shielded to reflect downward, be motion -activated, and be 
in conformance with the Navajo County Lighting Ordinance. 
 
4. A Site Lighting Plan prepared by an Electrical Engineer licensed in the State of Arizona 
(required by code for commercial projects) shall be submitted. 
 
5. A solid wall or fence, at least six feet in height, shall be required on the northern boundary of 
this Special Use site. 
 
6. Hours of operation will be 24 hours, 7 days a week, with an entrance gate with keypad. 
 
7. A Drainage Study, which meets the Navajo County Drainage Policy, shall be submitted and 
approved. 
 
8. A Grading and Drainage Plan shall be submitted and approved by Navajo County Public Works 
prior to the issuance of a building permit for any of the proposed structures. 
 
9. A Geotechnical Report for gravel roadway section shall be submitted and approved. 
 
10. A Navajo County Commercial Right-of-Way Permit must be acquired prior to paving the asphalt 
entrances. 
 
11. A copy of the Navajo County Commercial Right-of-Way Permit for the driveway connection to 
Silver Creek Drive shall be submitted to Engineering. 
 
12. The Fire Department shall approve the fire suppression design for the development. 
 
13. The permitted Special Use shall be allowed to occur per the attached/approved site plan.  Any 
expansion or change of the Special Use shall require an amendment to this permit. 
 
14. If the permitted Special Use is not utilized within a twelve (12) month period,  from the date of 
Board approval, this permit shall become null and void.

<!-- page 426 -->
Board of Supervisors              6 | P a g e  
 
 
15. This Special Use Permit shall be personal to the permittee and applicable only to the specific use and 
to the specific property for which it is issued. Upon completion and final ins pection by the Planning 
and Development Services Director (or designee) of any authorized structures, signifying that all 
zoning and site development requirements imposed in connection with the permit have been satisfied, 
the Special Use Permit shall there after be transferable and shall run with the land, whereupon the 
maintenance of special conditions imposed by the permit, as well as the compliance with other 
provisions of this title, shall be the responsibility of the property owner. 
 
PLANNING AND ZONING COMMISSION RECOMMENDATION: 
 
On April 20, 2023, the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission voted unanimously (8:0) to 
recommend approval of the White Mountain Lake Self Storage  project to the Navajo County Board of 
Supervisors.

Item 6. SUP 23-002 Kennison Contractor Yard,

Commissioner Murph motioned to approve the agenda with the reordered items. Commissioner Rogers

2. CALL TO PUBLIC FOR ITEMS NOT ON THE AGENDA: Individuals may address the Commission on any
relevant issue for up to 5 minutes. At the close of the call to the public, Commission members may not
respond to any comments but may respond to criticism, ask staff to review a matter or ask that a matter be

There were no public comments.
3. CONSIDERATION OF APPROVAL OF MINUTES FROM THE COMMISSION HEARING OF: March 16, 2023:
Commissioner Booher made a motion to approve the minutes. Commissioner Muder seconded the motion.
Motion passed unanimously, 8 - 0.
4. COMP 23-001 Update to Navajo County Comprehensive Plan: Consideration of an update to the
1 of 5 
DRAFT

<!-- page 439 -->
4. COMP 23-001 Update to Navajo County Comprehensive Plan: Consideration of an update to the
Comprehensive Plan of Navajo County:
Mr. Cody Cooper, Planning Manager, presented the item to the Commission.
The update would include:
  
A hearing process for proposing major amendments to the Navajo County Comprehensive Plan. Mr.
Cooper then gave a brief overview of how that process would function.
Updated population numbers and estimates to reflect the 2020 Census results, as well as more current
information and guidance provided from the Arizona Office of Economic Opportunity.
Formatting to make the Comprehensive Plan less complex and improve readability, remove redundancy,
and update the goals that have been met or need to be adjusted for clarity.
Mr. Cooper moved on to describing the public outreach Staff had done, a 60-day commitment period that
began in January. In total, 15 adjacent jurisdictions had been contacted, and three responded with questions,
but had no comments or concerns about the update. Two public meetings had been hosted, one in Holbrook
and the other in Show Low. No members of the public attended or provided comments at either meeting. With
this information, Staff recommends approval of this update.
There were no comments from the attending public about the Comprehensive Plan.
Commissioner Murph asked where the numbers presented were from. Mr. Cooper clarified that the models
were from the Arizona Office of Economic Opportunity, and that they were making projections for growth
based on their updated data, as the last models were flawed. 
Commissioner Murph continued, stating that even knowing that the projections were not set in stone, they
would still influence the County in some way. Mr. Cooper replied that they could, and that while they were
going to plan for the current citizens of the County, they also needed to be flexible to account for any changes
that may happen in the future.
With no further comments from the Commission or the public, Commissioner Murph made a motion to
recommend approval to the Board of Supervisors. Commissioner Rogers seconded the motion. Motion
passed, 8 - 0.
 
  
 
5. SUP 23-001 White Mountain Lake Self-Storage - District III:  Consideration of a request for a Special Use
Permit to develop a self-storage facility with four banks of new storage units on a vacant 1.2-acre parcel
within the White Mountain Lake area of Show Low:
Ms. Sondra Starke, Planner I, presented the item to the Commission. The request was for a self-storage
rental facility on Silver Creek Drive in White Mountain Lakes with four banks of storage. Ms. Starke showed
the Commission a map of the surrounding area and listed the nearby landmarks.  She then talked about the
features of the project that met development standards such as the ingress and egress, engineering, fire
safety requirements, drainage detention, etc.
The zoning for the property is Commercial-Residential (C-R) and does allow storage facilities with a Special
Use Permit. Staff had not received any comments from the public either for or against the project. With these
findings of fact, Staff recommended approval of the Special Use Permit.
The applicant, Mr. Joseph Holland, addressed the Commission. Mr. Holland said that the community was very
much in need of more storage facilities and felt that his project would help meet those demands.
Mr. Ronald Longenbaugh asked the Commission about the growth of the area and if there would be more
commercial uses, as he was under the impression the area was residential. He had no comments either for or
against the project.
Mr. Cody Cooper, Planning Manager, addressed Mr. Longenbaugh's question, showing the Commission and
Mr. Longenbaugh the areas that were zoned C-R and stated that it did have the potential for more commercial
uses.
With there being no further comments from either the public or the Commission, Commissioner Muder made
a motion to recommend approval to the Board. Commissioner Rogers seconded the motion. Motion passed
unanimously, 8 - 0.
  
 
6. SUP 23-002 Kennison Contractor Yard - District IV: Consideration of a request for a Special Use Permit to   
2 of 5 
DRAFT

<!-- page 440 -->
6. SUP 23-002 Kennison Contractor Yard - District IV: Consideration of a request for a Special Use Permit to
expand use of an existing primary dwelling to include indoor storage of contractor’s equipment on a 0.44-acre
parcel located in Overgaard. APN# 206-27-020, T12N, R17E, S33. Owner: Kennison Brian R & Silvia M
Family Revocable Trust. Agent: Brian Kennison:
Ms. Sondra Starke, Planner I, presented the item to the Commission. The property owner, Mr. Brian Kennison
was requesting a Special Use Permit (SUP) to add a use to his permitted single-family dwelling as a storage
garage for his landscaping business’ equipment. 
The property is just to the west of Bison Ranch, and the zoning for the property is R1-10. The zoning does
allow for contractor's yards, with the condition that they obtain an SUP. Ms. Starke further shared with the
Commission a site plan of the project and explained that the permitted structure would have an 800 square
foot living quarters inside of it, and then 1600 square feet for equipment storage. 
The use is compatible with the surrounding area, as the property is adjacent to C-R (Commercial Residential)
zoning along Highway 260. The project has been determined to be compatible with current and future uses
and fits in with the Comprehensive Plan. With these findings of fact, Staff recommended approval.
The applicant, Brian Kennison, addressed the Commission and thanked them for their time. He stated that the
reason they chose this property was because of the Assessor's web description of the list of allowed uses,
one of which was a contractor's yard, with an asterisk attached to it. Mr. Kennison also mentioned that the
location was ideal for his business as it was very close to Bison Ranch, which he had contracts with for 18
years to maintain their grounds and roads.
Mr. Kennison had not inquired further than the list of allowed uses and moved his equipment onto the
property. Following a complaint, he was informed by Code Enforcement that he was required to obtain a
Special Use Permit.
Vice-Chairwoman Smith opened the meeting for public comment.
There were no comments in favor of the project.
Comments against the project:
Sandy Kreston, Jeff Schwoerer, John Schusler, and Kim Johnston all had comments against the project,
including:
  
The yard was currently a mess, with equipment and materials scattered around, and had been that way
for two to three years.
Concern over code violations.
The lot did not have a home on it.
The current screening was not sufficient.
The building proposed would not be big enough to hold the equipment.
The business was bringing down property values.
Noise of employees and machinery.
Hours of Operation.
Invasive lighting.
Concern over who would be living on the property.
Concern over harmful chemicals being stored on the property.
Concern that the equipment would damage the roads and the easement used to access the property.
There would be gravel and dirt on the roads because of the equipment that made walking or biking
difficult.
Vice-Chairwoman Smith asked Mr. Kennison to address some of these comments.
Mr. Kennison responded:
  
The building would be arriving next Monday, and groundbreaking would begin in April, shielding the
closest neighbor's view from his back deck.
The yard would be screened with trees and landscaping, and the current screening was temporary.
His hours of operation for his business would be from 8:00 AM to 4:00 PM. Most work would be done
off-site, with employees only picking up equipment in the morning and returning it by the end of the day.
The easement in question was used by two other commercial businesses.
He would not be storing harmful chemicals or materials.
Mr. Kennison also commented that it was likely he would rent the living quarters to one of his
employees, of which he had two.
Current materials on the property were for the construction of the building and he would no longer be
storing materials on site once construction was complete.
Any damage to the roads because of the equipment would be repaired by his company, as he was
under contract with Bison Ranch to maintain them.
He would not be doing retail business on site.
  
3 of 5 
DRAFT

<!-- page 441 -->
Repairs would be done inside of the building, not outside. Mr. Kennison also had an off-site mechanic to
which would take the equipment.
The structure is permitted by Navajo County.
All the non-compliant equipment will be moved to a different property.
Vice-Chairwoman Smith asked about the easement being blocked. Mr. Kennison replied that the easement
back behind the auto body shop towards the Dollar General had been blocked for roughly ten years.
Vice-Chairwoman Smith also provided comments about the storing of materials and parking of private vehicles
on the easement close to the fire hydrant and requested that they be moved for public safety, to which Mr.
Kennison agreed.
Commissioner Rogers asked about any Code Enforcement violations. Mr. Cody Cooper, Planning Manager,
responded saying that there was an open Code Enforcement violation on the property, which is why Mr.
Kennison was applying for this SUP to resolve the issues. The violation was the outdoor storage of
contractors' materials, as well as the utilization of the contractors' yard.  Code Enforcement stated that the
applicant either needed to acquire an SUP or cease the usage in order to close the open violation. 
Commissioner Murph asked for clarification of the need for the SUP process, what was by-right usage, and
when the line was drawn to have to apply for an SUP, as the building permit had been issued. Mr. Cooper
noted that the hearing process is in place for Staff to be able to ensure that the use fits in with the
Comprehensive Plan, and that public interests and safety be considered in a public forum.
Mr. Mischa Larisch, Public Works Deputy Director of Planning & Development Services, approached the
Commission to state the reason Mr. Kennison’s use was not by-right was because he had outside employees
come to the property. Otherwise, if he had only his family running the business, he would be free to store the
equipment inside of his building without an SUP.
Commissioner Muder asked if storage containers were allowed in the R1-10 zoning. Mr Cooper replied that
they were allowed under certain parameters in the Zoning Ordinance.
There was more discussion regarding fencing, with Commissioner Rogers asking if they could add in
a stipulation that he fence the property. Mr. Cooper responded that the Commission could add in that
requirement but could only go so far as to require a solid fence, not material types.
Vice-Chairwoman Smith asked Staff to clarify the 12-month sunset clause on the SUP. Mr. Cooper
addressed this, stating that the applicant had 12 months to start construction, or the permit would expire.
Commissioner Howe made a motion to recommend approval to the Board, with an addition that a fence be
put in per staff recommendation to shield them from the neighbors on the east. Commissioner Rogers
seconded the motion. Motion passed, 7 - 1 (Commissioner Booher).
 
7. SUP-A 23-001 Evergreen Storage - District IV:  Consideration of a request for a Special Use Permit
Amendment from an RV Park and RV storage facility to a two-phase RV, trailer, and mini-storage facility on a
vacant 4.30-acre parcel in the Overgaard area.  APN# 206-02-012C, T12N, R17E, S30. Owner: Larry and
Dianne Holland. Applicant/Agent: Logan Rogers:
Both Vice-Chairwoman Smith and Commissioner Rogers temporarily recused themselves from the hearing
for this item, as they both had a conflict of interest in the project, with Commissioner Rogers being the
applicant and Vice-Chairwoman Smith being the representative for the real-estate transaction.
Per the bylaws of the Commission, members needed to elect a Chair Pro Tem to proceed with the meeting
and continue.
Commissioner Murph nominated Commissioner Howe as Chair Pro tem; Commissioner Muder seconded
the motion. The vote was unanimous, 6 - 0, for the election of Commissioner Howe as Pro tem.
With this business concluded, Mr. Cody Cooper, Planning Manager, presented the item to the Commission,
giving them a brief history of the project, stating that this was an amendment to an existing Special Use Permit
that had been approved by the Board of Supervisors in October of 2022. 
The approval was given to the current landowner, with a different applicant, with the condition that the project
be started within twelve (12) months or the permit would expire. This meant that the previous approval was
still active. The current applicant was requesting an amendment with their own proposal for the property,
which would have two phases. The original approval was for 20 to 25 RV Park sites and 20 storage units. 
The amended use proposes that Phase One be 200 self-storage units with 44 RV and trailer storage spaces.
Phase two would then remove the RV and trailer storage, add an additional 100 storage units, and add a
commercial center. Mr. Cooper showed the area of notification for this amendment, noting that there was no
public response either for or against the project. Staff recommended approval of the amendment.
Applicant, Logan Rogers, addressed the Commission, stating that the reason he took on this project was to
  
4 of 5 
DRAFT

<!-- page 442 -->
Applicant, Logan Rogers, addressed the Commission, stating that the reason he took on this project was to
serve a need for more storage facilities in the community as the current storage facilities in the area are at full
capacity and have been so for years. His reason for requesting the two-phase project was to minimize the
need to amend another permit, as they were planning for future expansion.
With there being no further comments from the Commission or the public, Commissioner Muder made a
motion to recommend approval to the Board. Commissioner Murph seconded the motion. Motion carried
unanimously, 6 - 0.

recommend approval of this Special Use Permit, subject to conditions.

STAFF REPORT  
BOARD OF SUPERVISORS 
 
Hearing Date: May 23, 2023 
Case No:  SUP 23-002 
Action:   Special Use Permit  
Applicant/Owner: Brian Kennison 
Project Name: Kennison Contractor Yard 
EXECUTIVE SUMMARY 
Recommendation: Staff, as well as the Planning and Zoning Commission, recommend approval of 
the Special Use Permit, subject to the conditions of this Staff Report. 
 
Location: The property is located in Overgaard. The parcel is identified as APN 206-27-020.  
 
Size: One (1) parcel totaling approximately 0.44 acres. 
 
Supervisor District: IV. 
 
Zoning: R1-10 (Residential Zoning District, 10,000 Square Feet per Dwelling Unit). 
 
Property Owners: Kennison Brian R & Sylvia M Family Revocable Trust. 
 
Applicant: Brian Kennison. 
 
Representative: Brian Kennison. 
 
REQUEST DESCRIPTION:  
A request for a Special Use Permit to expand use of an existing primary dwelling to include indoor 
storage of contractor’s equipment on a 0.44-acre parcel located in Overgaard. 
 
GENERAL CHARACTER OF NEIGHBORHOOD:  
 
The majority of the land in the vicinity of this project is zoned R1-10.  Additionally, a section of C -R 
zoning exists along Hwy 260, bordering the project site on the north and west sides. 
 
BACKGROUND/HISTORY:

<!-- page 457 -->
Board of Supervisors             2 | P a g e  
 
A building permit has been issued by the Navajo County Building Division for the single -family 
dwelling included in this project. Construction of the building has commenced, and the septic system 
has been installed as permitted.  
 
All business operations of the contractor, Integrity Landscape, Inc., will be conducted off -site of the 
project as the property will be used exclusively for equipment storage and residential usage. 
 
PROJECT DESCRIPTION:  
 
This project is designed to contain the following components: 
 
- Forty by forty-foot (40’x40’) building with eight -hundred square feet (800 sq. ft.) livable area 
and one thousand-two hundred square feet (1200 sq. ft.) storage area 
- Six (6) foot tall no-climb wire fence to be completed with wrapped screen no-view fence 
- Landscaped with evergreen along fence line 
- Basalt driveway and gravel yard 
 
ZONING DISTRICTS: 
ARTICLE  11 – (R1-10) – RESIDENTIAL ZONING DISTRICT 
 
Section 1101 – Purpose  
 
The principal purpose of this zoning district is to conserve and protect single -family residential development 
where minimum lots of not less than ten thousand (10,000) square feet in area are suitable and appropriate 
taking into consideration existing conditions, including present use of land, present lot sizes, future land use 
needs, and the availability of public utilities. Principal uses permitted in this zo ning district include single -
family dwellings, churches, schools, parks, playgrounds, and other community facilities. 
 
ZONING ORDINANCE: 
Article 20 – Special Uses and Planned Unit Developments 
 
Section 2001 – Special Uses 
  
The Board of Supervisors may per mit as a Special Use the following uses in zoning districts 
from which they are otherwise prohibited by this Ordinance, unless any such use is further 
prohibited by this Article. 
 
 8. Contractor’s Yard – Provided that no special use permit shall be granted unless the 
parcel is in the A-General, rural, or residential zoning district, and provided further that 
the area used as a storage area for materials or supplies shall be screened fro m adjacent 
properties by natural or man-made means at least six (6) feet in height. 
 
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN/AREA PLAN:  
Comprehensive Plan

<!-- page 458 -->
Board of Supervisors             3 | P a g e  
 
Land Use Element 
 
Goal 6: Maintain compatible land use patterns while encouraging free market development. 
 
 Policy 6-B: Consider, if needed, providing for buffers between different land uses including, 
 but not limited to, setbacks, walls or fences, and landscaping. 
 
- The applicant has indicated that all equipment will be stored inside of the project building, 
which provides a buffer between the surrounding residential use pattern and the contractor’s 
yard usage of the project. Additionally, the applicant has indicated that a screening method 
will be implemented to further buffer visibility of the project use.  
 
Heber/Overgaard Area Plan 
 
There are no relevant goals or objectives in the Heber/Overgaard Area Plan for this project.  
 
Analysis: 
 
This project meets, or intends to meet, the aforementioned goal of the Land Use Element of the Navajo 
County Comprehensive Plan. The applicant has already worked ahead to have a plan for any screening 
and buffer requirements for the project boundary.  
 
The Heber/Overgaard Area Plan has indicated that it supports contractor’s yard projects within this 
Planning Area but contains no specific goals that are applicable to this project.  
 
Planning and Development Services Staff have received two (2) phone call s and six (6) emails  in 
opposition of this project as of May 10, 2023. 
 
FINDINGS OF FACT: 
 
1. The item has been properly noticed and posted in compliance with Arizona Revised Statutes 
and Article 29.  
 
This item was posted in the May 5th, 2023, edition of the White Mountain Independent.  Postcards 
were mailed to neighbors within  300 feet of the property. The property was posted with public 
hearing notices.  
 
2. The proposal is in compliance with the Comprehensive Plan. 
 
The proposed project meets a ll applicable goals within Navajo County’s Comprehensive Plan, as 
noted within this report. 
 
3. The proposed use is compatible with the permissible uses in the zoning district in which the 
property is located. 
 
The proposed use is not allowed by -right in the zoning distri ct in which the property is located, 
which is why a Special Use Permit is being requested.

<!-- page 459 -->
Board of Supervisors             4 | P a g e  
 
4. The proposed use is  compatible with the current and likely future uses of properties in the 
vicinity of the proposed use.  
 
The proposed use has been determined to be compatible with current and likely future uses per the 
Navajo County Zoning Ordinance.  
 
5. The proposed use will have minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general 
welfare of properties in the vicinity of the proposed use. 
 
Given the nature of the property and the current makeup of the nearby landscape, there should be 
minimal adverse effects on the public health, safety, and general welfare of properties in the vicinity 
of the proposed use.

PLANNING AND DEVELOPMENT SERVICES RECOMMENDATION:  
 
Navajo County Planning and Development Services Staff recommend approval of the Special Use Permit, 
subject to the following conditions: 
 
1. The applicant shall obtain any required Navajo County permits including but not limited to 
building, grading, drainage, and sign permits.  
 
2. No building or structure shall be occupied prior to compliance with all appropriate Building 
Division requirements.  
 
3. All lighting on the property shall be shielded to reflect downward, be motion activated, and be 
in conformance with the Navajo County Lighting Ordinance.  
 
4. If there is any outdoor storage, a solid wall or fence at least six (6) feet in height shall be required 
adjacent to any residential properties.  
 
5. The permitted special use shall be allowed to occur per the attached/approved site plan. Any 
expansion or change of the special use shall require an amendment to this permit.  
 
6. If the permitted special use i s not utilized within a twelve (12) month period from the date of

<!-- page 460 -->
Board of Supervisors             5 | P a g e  
 
board approval, this permit shall become null and void.  
 
7. This Special Use Permit shall be personal to the permittee and applicable only to the specific 
use and to the specific property for which it is issued. Upon completion and final inspection by 
the Planning and Development Services Director (or designee) of any authorized structures, 
signifying that all zoning and site development requirements imposed in connection with the 
permit have been satisfied, the Special Use Permit shall thereafter be transferable and shall run 
with the land, whereupon the maintenance of special conditions imposed by the permit, as well 
as the compliance with other provisions of this title, shall be the responsib ility of the property 
owner.  
 
PLANNING AND ZONING COMMISSION RECOMMENDATION: 
 
On April 20, 2023, the Navajo County Planning and Zoning Commission voted (7:1) to recommend approval 
of Kennison Contractor’s Yard to the Navajo County Board of Supervisors, with the addition of the following 
condition: 
 
1. The applicant must screen the east side of the property with a solid fence at least six feet (6’) in height.

4. COMP 23-001 Update to Navajo County Comprehensive Plan: Consideration of an update to the
1 of 5 
DRAFT

<!-- page 473 -->
4. COMP 23-001 Update to Navajo County Comprehensive Plan: Consideration of an update to the
Comprehensive Plan of Navajo County:
Mr. Cody Cooper, Planning Manager, presented the item to the Commission.
The update would include:
  
A hearing process for proposing major amendments to the Navajo County Comprehensive Plan. Mr.
Cooper then gave a brief overview of how that process would function.
Updated population numbers and estimates to reflect the 2020 Census results, as well as more current
information and guidance provided from the Arizona Office of Economic Opportunity.
Formatting to make the Comprehensive Plan less complex and improve readability, remove redundancy,
and update the goals that have been met or need to be adjusted for clarity.
Mr. Cooper moved on to describing the public outreach Staff had done, a 60-day commitment period that
began in January. In total, 15 adjacent jurisdictions had been contacted, and three responded with questions,
but had no comments or concerns about the update. Two public meetings had been hosted, one in Holbrook
and the other in Show Low. No members of the public attended or provided comments at either meeting. With
this information, Staff recommends approval of this update.
There were no comments from the attending public about the Comprehensive Plan.
Commissioner Murph asked where the numbers presented were from. Mr. Cooper clarified that the models
were from the Arizona Office of Economic Opportunity, and that they were making projections for growth
based on their updated data, as the last models were flawed. 
Commissioner Murph continued, stating that even knowing that the projections were not set in stone, they
would still influence the County in some way. Mr. Cooper replied that they could, and that while they were
going to plan for the current citizens of the County, they also needed to be flexible to account for any changes
that may happen in the future.
With no further comments from the Commission or the public, Commissioner Murph made a motion to
recommend approval to the Board of Supervisors. Commissioner Rogers seconded the motion. Motion
passed, 8 - 0.
 
  
 
5. SUP 23-001 White Mountain Lake Self-Storage - District III:  Consideration of a request for a Special Use
Permit to develop a self-storage facility with four banks of new storage units on a vacant 1.2-acre parcel
within the White Mountain Lake area of Show Low:
Ms. Sondra Starke, Planner I, presented the item to the Commission. The request was for a self-storage
rental facility on Silver Creek Drive in White Mountain Lakes with four banks of storage. Ms. Starke showed
the Commission a map of the surrounding area and listed the nearby landmarks.  She then talked about the
features of the project that met development standards such as the ingress and egress, engineering, fire
safety requirements, drainage detention, etc.
The zoning for the property is Commercial-Residential (C-R) and does allow storage facilities with a Special
Use Permit. Staff had not received any comments from the public either for or against the project. With these
findings of fact, Staff recommended approval of the Special Use Permit.
The applicant, Mr. Joseph Holland, addressed the Commission. Mr. Holland said that the community was very
much in need of more storage facilities and felt that his project would help meet those demands.
Mr. Ronald Longenbaugh asked the Commission about the growth of the area and if there would be more
commercial uses, as he was under the impression the area was residential. He had no comments either for or
against the project.
Mr. Cody Cooper, Planning Manager, addressed Mr. Longenbaugh's question, showing the Commission and
Mr. Longenbaugh the areas that were zoned C-R and stated that it did have the potential for more commercial
uses.
With there being no further comments from either the public or the Commission, Commissioner Muder made
a motion to recommend approval to the Board. Commissioner Rogers seconded the motion. Motion passed
unanimously, 8 - 0.
  
 
6. SUP 23-002 Kennison Contractor Yard - District IV: Consideration of a request for a Special Use Permit to   
2 of 5 
DRAFT

<!-- page 474 -->
6. SUP 23-002 Kennison Contractor Yard - District IV: Consideration of a request for a Special Use Permit to
expand use of an existing primary dwelling to include indoor storage of contractor’s equipment on a 0.44-acre
parcel located in Overgaard. APN# 206-27-020, T12N, R17E, S33. Owner: Kennison Brian R & Silvia M
Family Revocable Trust. Agent: Brian Kennison:
Ms. Sondra Starke, Planner I, presented the item to the Commission. The property owner, Mr. Brian Kennison
was requesting a Special Use Permit (SUP) to add a use to his permitted single-family dwelling as a storage
garage for his landscaping business’ equipment. 
The property is just to the west of Bison Ranch, and the zoning for the property is R1-10. The zoning does
allow for contractor's yards, with the condition that they obtain an SUP. Ms. Starke further shared with the
Commission a site plan of the project and explained that the permitted structure would have an 800 square
foot living quarters inside of it, and then 1600 square feet for equipment storage. 
The use is compatible with the surrounding area, as the property is adjacent to C-R (Commercial Residential)
zoning along Highway 260. The project has been determined to be compatible with current and future uses
and fits in with the Comprehensive Plan. With these findings of fact, Staff recommended approval.
The applicant, Brian Kennison, addressed the Commission and thanked them for their time. He stated that the
reason they chose this property was because of the Assessor's web description of the list of allowed uses,
one of which was a contractor's yard, with an asterisk attached to it. Mr. Kennison also mentioned that the
location was ideal for his business as it was very close to Bison Ranch, which he had contracts with for 18
years to maintain their grounds and roads.
Mr. Kennison had not inquired further than the list of allowed uses and moved his equipment onto the
property. Following a complaint, he was informed by Code Enforcement that he was required to obtain a
Special Use Permit.
Vice-Chairwoman Smith opened the meeting for public comment.
There were no comments in favor of the project.
Comments against the project:
Sandy Kreston, Jeff Schwoerer, John Schusler, and Kim Johnston all had comments against the project,
including:
  
The yard was currently a mess, with equipment and materials scattered around, and had been that way
for two to three years.
Concern over code violations.
The lot did not have a home on it.
The current screening was not sufficient.
The building proposed would not be big enough to hold the equipment.
The business was bringing down property values.
Noise of employees and machinery.
Hours of Operation.
Invasive lighting.
Concern over who would be living on the property.
Concern over harmful chemicals being stored on the property.
Concern that the equipment would damage the roads and the easement used to access the property.
There would be gravel and dirt on the roads because of the equipment that made walking or biking
difficult.
Vice-Chairwoman Smith asked Mr. Kennison to address some of these comments.
Mr. Kennison responded:
  
The building would be arriving next Monday, and groundbreaking would begin in April, shielding the
closest neighbor's view from his back deck.
The yard would be screened with trees and landscaping, and the current screening was temporary.
His hours of operation for his business would be from 8:00 AM to 4:00 PM. Most work would be done
off-site, with employees only picking up equipment in the morning and returning it by the end of the day.
The easement in question was used by two other commercial businesses.
He would not be storing harmful chemicals or materials.
Mr. Kennison also commented that it was likely he would rent the living quarters to one of his
employees, of which he had two.
Current materials on the property were for the construction of the building and he would no longer be
storing materials on site once construction was complete.
Any damage to the roads because of the equipment would be repaired by his company, as he was
under contract with Bison Ranch to maintain them.
He would not be doing retail business on site.
  
3 of 5 
DRAFT

<!-- page 475 -->
Repairs would be done inside of the building, not outside. Mr. Kennison also had an off-site mechanic to
which would take the equipment.
The structure is permitted by Navajo County.
All the non-compliant equipment will be moved to a different property.
Vice-Chairwoman Smith asked about the easement being blocked. Mr. Kennison replied that the easement
back behind the auto body shop towards the Dollar General had been blocked for roughly ten years.
Vice-Chairwoman Smith also provided comments about the storing of materials and parking of private vehicles
on the easement close to the fire hydrant and requested that they be moved for public safety, to which Mr.
Kennison agreed.
Commissioner Rogers asked about any Code Enforcement violations. Mr. Cody Cooper, Planning Manager,
responded saying that there was an open Code Enforcement violation on the property, which is why Mr.
Kennison was applying for this SUP to resolve the issues. The violation was the outdoor storage of
contractors' materials, as well as the utilization of the contractors' yard.  Code Enforcement stated that the
applicant either needed to acquire an SUP or cease the usage in order to close the open violation. 
Commissioner Murph asked for clarification of the need for the SUP process, what was by-right usage, and
when the line was drawn to have to apply for an SUP, as the building permit had been issued. Mr. Cooper
noted that the hearing process is in place for Staff to be able to ensure that the use fits in with the
Comprehensive Plan, and that public interests and safety be considered in a public forum.
Mr. Mischa Larisch, Public Works Deputy Director of Planning & Development Services, approached the
Commission to state the reason Mr. Kennison’s use was not by-right was because he had outside employees
come to the property. Otherwise, if he had only his family running the business, he would be free to store the
equipment inside of his building without an SUP.
Commissioner Muder asked if storage containers were allowed in the R1-10 zoning. Mr Cooper replied that
they were allowed under certain parameters in the Zoning Ordinance.
There was more discussion regarding fencing, with Commissioner Rogers asking if they could add in
a stipulation that he fence the property. Mr. Cooper responded that the Commission could add in that
requirement but could only go so far as to require a solid fence, not material types.
Vice-Chairwoman Smith asked Staff to clarify the 12-month sunset clause on the SUP. Mr. Cooper
addressed this, stating that the applicant had 12 months to start construction, or the permit would expire.
Commissioner Howe made a motion to recommend approval to the Board, with an addition that a fence be
put in per staff recommendation to shield them from the neighbors on the east. Commissioner Rogers
seconded the motion. Motion passed, 7 - 1 (Commissioner Booher).
 
7. SUP-A 23-001 Evergreen Storage - District IV:  Consideration of a request for a Special Use Permit
Amendment from an RV Park and RV storage facility to a two-phase RV, trailer, and mini-storage facility on a
vacant 4.30-acre parcel in the Overgaard area.  APN# 206-02-012C, T12N, R17E, S30. Owner: Larry and
Dianne Holland. Applicant/Agent: Logan Rogers:
Both Vice-Chairwoman Smith and Commissioner Rogers temporarily recused themselves from the hearing
for this item, as they both had a conflict of interest in the project, with Commissioner Rogers being the
applicant and Vice-Chairwoman Smith being the representative for the real-estate transaction.
Per the bylaws of the Commission, members needed to elect a Chair Pro Tem to proceed with the meeting
and continue.
Commissioner Murph nominated Commissioner Howe as Chair Pro tem; Commissioner Muder seconded
the motion. The vote was unanimous, 6 - 0, for the election of Commissioner Howe as Pro tem.
With this business concluded, Mr. Cody Cooper, Planning Manager, presented the item to the Commission,
giving them a brief history of the project, stating that this was an amendment to an existing Special Use Permit
that had been approved by the Board of Supervisors in October of 2022. 
The approval was given to the current landowner, with a different applicant, with the condition that the project
be started within twelve (12) months or the permit would expire. This meant that the previous approval was
still active. The current applicant was requesting an amendment with their own proposal for the property,
which would have two phases. The original approval was for 20 to 25 RV Park sites and 20 storage units. 
The amended use proposes that Phase One be 200 self-storage units with 44 RV and trailer storage spaces.
Phase two would then remove the RV and trailer storage, add an additional 100 storage units, and add a
commercial center. Mr. Cooper showed the area of notification for this amendment, noting that there was no
public response either for or against the project. Staff recommended approval of the amendment.
Applicant, Logan Rogers, addressed the Commission, stating that the reason he took on this project was to
  
4 of 5 
DRAFT

<!-- page 476 -->
Applicant, Logan Rogers, addressed the Commission, stating that the reason he took on this project was to
serve a need for more storage facilities in the community as the current storage facilities in the area are at full
capacity and have been so for years. His reason for requesting the two-phase project was to minimize the
need to amend another permit, as they were planning for future expansion.
With there being no further comments from the Commission or the public, Commissioner Muder made a
motion to recommend approval to the Board. Commissioner Murph seconded the motion. Motion carried
unanimously, 6 - 0.

Commissions’ choosing.
